1094. GEORGE SCULTHORPE and ISAAC LEVY were indicted for stealing, on the 20th of March, 1 looking-glass frame, value 30s., the goods of William Crawford Boone; and that the said Isaac Levy had been before convicted of felony.
JAMES BENNETT. I am eleven years old; I live withmy father and mother, in Fox-court, Gray's Inn-lane - I go to school and to church. On the 20th of March I saw the two prisoners at the corner of Fox-court; Sculthorpe remained there, and Levy went and took a looking-glass off a table in Mr. Boone's shop, which is about twenty yards off; I could see him do that from the corner of the court - he then went to Sculthorpe, and they dropped the glass between them, and broke the frame; they then picked up each of them a half, and ran down Fox-court, and into Feather's-court - I called Stop thief! but there was no one in the way; I lost them then, but I saw them in custody a week afterwards, and knew them; I knew them by sight before.
Levy. He said at the examination he would not swear we were the persons, as he only saw the back of them. Witness. No, I did not; I said it was these men - I saw both their faces; I did not go to the prosecutor at first, because I thought I would see where the prisoners went.
MARY KING. I am a widow, and live in Fox-court. I saw two men in Feather's-court; Sculthorpe was one of them, and he had part of a glass in his hand - the other man was in dark clothes, but I cannot be certain of his person.
Sculthorpe. On the first examination she said she saw a man with a flannel jacket. Witness. No, I said I saw you with part of the glass in your hand; I pointed you out, but did not know your name.
SUSANNAH MARIA BOONE. I am the wife of William Crawford Boone. On the 20th of March a dressing-glass was taken from our shop; I saw it safe about three o'clock; it was cut from a string in the shop - Bennett told me of it, and told me who the parties were.
Levy. Q. Was there not a young man who told you before Bennett? A. Yes, but he did not know who you were.
RICHARD BAYLIS (Police-constable G 67.) I apprehended the prisoners on the 27th of March, at the Three Tuns in Brook-street; they beat and kicked me about very much, and said if they were at liberty they would knife me - there were between twenty and thirty very bad characters in the house; my hat was twice cut through with a poker - we were obliged to send to the station for assistance before they could be got out, and this knife was thrown open into the crowd.
MOSES DAVIS. I am a constable. I was present at the trial of Levy in this Court, and I produce a copy of his former conviction - (read) - I know he is the man.
SCULTHORPE - GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
LEVY - GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18320405-292]
1095. CHARLES SMITH and THOMAS HORTON were indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of February, 1 cloak, value 28s., the goods of John Fenwick.
HENRY HENMAN. I am shopman to Mr. John Fenwick, linen-draper, Regent-circus. On the 22nd of February Mr. Coxhead informed us, that he saw two persons near our shop; I looked, and saw Horton, and a boy in a flannel jacket, pass our window once or twice; they appeared to be looking if any body watched them; I laid down behind the counter, and watched - there was a cloak hanging at our door - in about ten minutes I missed it; Horton and the other were gone - I then ran out, and saw Horton on the right side of the door; he had before been on the left -I then saw Smith, whom I had not seen before, rolling up his apron; I secured him, and found this cloak in his apron - Horton had ran off.
SAMUEL WOOD. I am shopman to the prosecutor. I came into the shop about ten o'clock, and saw Horton was the window several times; I got into a pulpit desk, and saw him go across the road - I got down and crept to the counter, behind some goods; I looked towards the door -I saw the cloak raised from a book, but I could not see who took it; I followed Horton down several streets - I called Stop thief! some persons secured him, and gave him to me; I took him to the shop, and there saw Smith.
WILLIAM DAWES. (Police-constable T 35) I received the prisoners from the two witnesses; I had before that seen them watching the shop, and when I returned back, they were given to me - Smith had the cloak in his apron, but denied knowing the other prisoner; he said he had committed the offence, and he must suffer for it.
Smith put in a written Defence, pleading distress - a witness, who gave him a good character, engaged to take him into his service.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 25.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.
HORTON - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320405-293]
1096. ELIZA RILEY was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of March, 1 watch, value 2l., the goods of Benjamin Cornish.
BENJAMIN CORNISH. I live in Manor-street, Chelsea, and am a private watchman at Carlton-terrace - I have known the prisoner about three months; I met her in the Broadway, Westminster, on Monday fortnight; I went home with her to Union-court - we went to bed; I had put my watch under the pillow - I did not then know that the prisoner was a soldier's wife; I fell asleep, and when I awoke the prisoner was gone; I missed my watch - I had my dog tied up in the room; I am quite sure he would not let any one have come into the room - he would not let the Policeman in without me; I had given the prisoner 1s., which was all she asked me - my watch was a silver one; I had paid 3l. 13s. 6d. for it the Saturday before.
Prisoner. There was another girl in the room, and it was her he spoke to. Witness. No, there was no other girl in the room - I will swear you had the watch; I did not go home with the other girl.
WILLIAM CRUSH. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Museum-street. On the 29th of March a silver watch was pawned with me by Thompson, in company with another female, who I believe was the prisoner - it was pawned in the name of Fitzmorris, in which name Thompson had been in the habit of pawning.
ELLEN THOMPSON. My husband is a labourer, and I sell things in the street. I met the prisoner in the street last Monday fortnight, and took her with me to the pawnbroker's, where I was then going; we there pawned the watch, and the prisoner bought a shawl, which is here - I gave the duplicate to her.
MR. CRUSH. This is the shawl I sold; the watch wastaken out on the Wednesday, and has not been found - these are the duplicates of it; it was a silver watch, jewelled; the maker's name was Wilks.
BENJAMIN CORNISH. My watch was No. 535; the maker's name was Wilks - here is the bill of it.
ROBERT GOOSE (Police-constable B 55.) I took the prisoner at Charing-cross - I found this shawl on her.
ELIZA MOXHAM (a prisoner.) On the Monday morning in question I went with the prisoner down the Broadway; we met the prosecutor, and went and had some half-and-half; he offered her 1s. to go home with her - we went home; she and the prosecutor went up stairs - when she came away from him, in about an hour and a half, she showed me a silver watch; she opened it, and I saw two fives and a three; I cannot read much, but I can tell figures - the figures on this paper (looking at one) are 1831; I saw the prisoner go down the court with a man and Thompson.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18320405-294]
1097. EMMA MARIA SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of March, 12 yards of ribbon, value 5s., the goods of Thomas Prall.
CHARLES GURR. I am shopman to Mr. Thomas Prall, a linen-draper, in Tottenham-court-road. On the morning of the 30th of March the prisoner came to the shop, with another young woman; I served them with three-quarters of a yard of ribbon; I then saw the prisoner put her hand to her bosom - I missed a roll of ribbon; I had counted the rolls before I showed them to them; I followed them out of the shop; they turned a street, and began to run; I called them back, and the prisoner produced this roll of ribbon, and a remnant; there were twelve yards of ribbon, worth 5s.; I gave them both in charge, but the other was discharged.
Prisoner. You did not see me put it into my bosom. Witness. I saw your hand in your bosom as I came from the window.
JOHN LANDON (Police-constable E 150.) I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The other girl took it, and put it into my bosom, under my shawl - I walked round the corner; I gave the ribbon out of my hand to the witness.
JOHN LANGHAN. I went last night to her mistress, who keeps a respectable public-house; she is not able to attend, but stated that the prisoner had lived with her three times, and she will take her again.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18320405-295]
1098. JOHN SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of March, 3 breasts of mutton, value 2s., the goods of Edward Kimber.
GEORGE FORD (Police-constable F 50.) Mr. Kimber, who is a butcher, and lives in Bell-yard, Temple-bar, applied to me to watch his shop, on Saturday night, the 11th of March, and about half-past three o'clock on the Sunday morning, I saw the prisoner at the bottom of Bellyard - he crossed, and went into Apollo-court; I went and knocked at the prosecutor's door, and they let me in - I had been in about ten minutes or a quarter of an hour; the prisoner then came; he got on the ledge outside, and put his arm into the shop - in a few minutes a match broke out on fire, and he could see every thing that was in the shop; I went towards the front door, on my knees - he had then got down, and got up again with a stick and a hook at the end - he fixed the hook into a breast of mutton, and drew it towards him, but it came off, and dropped into the shop- he then took another, but that also dropped in the shop; he then took a third, and that he got out - I opened the door, and pursued him with the stick and mutton in his hand; he ran up Apollo-court - I lost sight of him for a moment, but two City watchmen came up; I went into a house into which the prisoner had gone, and met him coming down stairs - the stick and the meat he had put on the stairs.
Prisoner. Q. How far up the stairs did you find the meat? A. About four stairs up the second flight, where you returned to meet me; I had not lost sight of you half a minute - I did not say I did not know you without taking you to the light; the two watchmen had lights - I had not.
EDWARD KIMBER. I am a butcher. I employed the officer to watch my shop; I saw a man put his arm through into my shop, and a match burst out into a light - he got down, and got up again; I saw the stick and the book stuck in the meat - I did not see his face - I was watching in the adjoining room; I have been repeatedly robbed.
Prisoner's Defence. I never was on the stairs - I was out of doors when he took me.
GUILTY. Aged 30. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320405-296]

1100. THOMAS TOOMEY was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 2s. 6d., the goods of John Kiser.
MARGARET KISER. I am the wife of John Kiser - we live in George-street, Commercial-road - I am a laundress. On the 10th of March the prisoner came to my house, and while he was there a woman brought in some things, which she had been mangling; she put them in a chair, and the prisoner sat down on them - he rustled about a good deal, and I wanted to know what he was about; I put him out of the chair, and when he was gone I missed this handkerchief, which should have been among the things, but I had not seen it in the chair - I went to the prisoner's father, and told him; the prisoner came to my house, and said he knew nothing at all about it, but he afterwards brought the duplicate - I went and got it out of pawn; this is it - I got it by means of the duplicate he gave me; it was one I had washed, and my husband is answerable for it.
Prisoner. She said she would give me 6d. if I went and got the duplicate. Witness. He asked me 6d., and I gave it him.
MARY SAMWOYS. I am the wife of John Samwoys. I took the things which I had mangled to Mrs. Kiser's; this handkerchief was among them - I did not go in, nor see the prisoner; the handkerchief was marked J.K. - I took them home on the Saturday.
Prisoner's Defence. There was a little girl in thehouse; she knocked at the door, and said, "My mother is gone out - will you let me come and have some tea with you?" she sat down to tea, and I came away.
MRS. KISER. It was pawned in High-street, Poplar - the prisoner said he had not pawned it, nor had he the duplicate - he had given it to a girl who was with him, and he gave her 6d.
WILLIAM CLAYTON (Police-constable K 138.) I took the prisoner in Cross-street, Commercial-road.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.

1101. JOHN WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 19 pairs of stockings, value 19s., the goods of John Mills.
GEORGE CARTER. I am shopman to John Mills, a hosier, in Oxford-street. I was in the street on the night of the 3rd of April, about six yards from the door - I saw the prisoner take from the door two bundles of stockings, and run away; I took him with them.
GEORGE MILLS (Police-constable D 84.) I took the prisoner, and found on him four duplicates, a knife, two newspapers, a chain, a seal, and a pair of black stockings; he appeared in distress.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw them taken, and as I was crossing the road they were given to me; I received them from Samuel Jones - I was in distress.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Confined Three Months.

1102. MARY ANN WEST was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April, 1 shawl, value 10s., 2 gowns, value 10s., and 1 bonnet, value 5s., the goods of Sarah Coward.
SARAH COWARD. I am single, and live in the Almonry, Westminster. The prisoner lodged in my house on the the 3rd of April; when I got up about seven o'clock. I missed her, and found the door open - I looked about, and found my box had been opened with a key; I missed a shawl, two gowns, and a straw bonnet - I had seen them safe the night before, when I went to bed; I found the prisoner the same night at Knightsbridge, with my bonnet on her head - I asked how she came to take the things; she denied it at first - I said how came she to deny it when she had my bonnet on, and then she said she had left the things at a public-house; I found them there - there were my keys, another key, an old gown, and my shawl.
JOHN TAYLOR. I am shopman to a pawnbroker at Knightsbridge. I have a shawl, pawned by the prisoner, on the 3rd of April; the gown was offered after I had information of the robbery - I detained her with it.
WILLIAM CLIFTON (Police-constable B 50.) I apprehended the prisoner with this bonnet on her head, and she referred me to No. 17, Kennington-street; she said some of the prosecutor's things were left there, which were an apron and a lace cap, and several other things which were delivered up at the public-house, at Knights-bridge; I found this basket and the keys.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.

1103. MARY WAREING was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of March, 1 saucepan, value 6d., the goods of James Deadman.
JAMES DEADMAN. I live in Golden-lane, and am a pewterer. On the 29th of March I was in my parlour -I saw the prisoner in the yard, stooping as if to pick up something; I told my wife, and she went to her - I saw her with this saucepan in her hand, which is mine - I had seen it about ten minutes before; it was full of soap suds.
SARAH DEADMAN. My husband told me, and I saw the prisoner with this saucepan in her hand.
THOMAS SEAL (Police-constable G 145). I received the prisoner; she said at the station, that she saw Mother Walker, whom she had not seen for three months before; they went and had a drop of gin, and she asked Walker to lend her a saucepan; that she told her "That is my house, go in and take one;" as we were going along, a person came up and said, "Mother Wareing, what is this about?" she said, "It is through Mother Walker."(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 41.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Fourteen Days.

1104. JOHN BROOKS was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 2 glazed sashes, value 5s., the goods of William Humphries.
THOMAS CARTWRIGHT. I live at No. 1, John's-place, Old-street-road, and was in the employ of Mr. Rawlings, a timber dealer, in Old-street-road. The prisoner came there and asked us to buy two pairs of cucumber lights, and on the Saturday night my master did not agree to buy them, but he asked to leave them there till Monday; he did not come again till Wednesday morning - I then told him that they had been claimed; he said they were his own, and proposed to take them away; I said, I heard a gentleman had lost them, and till the gentleman came I should not part with them; he then asked if I would purchase them - I said No, unless my master consented.
WILLIAM HUMPHRIES. I keep the Marquis of Lansdown, in Thomas-street, Hackney-road. I missed these on Saturday week, but they were taken to the witness two or three days before that - my premises are a quarter of a mile from Mr. Rawlings.
GEORGE AVERY (Police-constable G 175.) I took the prisoner; he said he knew nothing about them.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had purchased the articles of a man he had worked with.
GUILTY. Aged 40. - Confined Six Months.
There was another indictment against the prisoner.

1105. HANNAH BURKE was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 2 shawls, value 25s.; 2 caps, value 3s.; 3 handkerchiefs, value 10s.; 1 gown, value 1s. 6d., and 1 pair of gloves, value 6d., the goods of William Chard, her master.
WILLIAM CHARD. I live in Little Queen-street, Edgware-road. The prisoner had been my servant about three months; between four and five o'clock in the morning of the 4th of January she left, and we missed these articles; in consequence of information, about three weeks after, we went to No. 64, South Molton-street, and the prisoner came to the door with my wife's gown on; I left her in charge - the night before she left us, my wife found herin bed with some person; she ran up to me, but when I got there, the person was gone; I went round the house at one o'clock, and there was no person there but the prisoner, who was asleep, and between four and five she came to our room for a light.
WILLIAM BLOOMFIELD (Police-constable D 9.) I went to No. 64, South Molton-street; the prisoner opened the door - the prosecutor said he wanted to speak to her; she made no answer; we went into the passage, and then took her up stairs, and the mistress claimed a shawl, a gown, and a cap, which she had on; Mr. Chard lost some money and a reticule - this is the shawl; the prisoner tore up a gown of her own before me and the prosecutor.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320405-303]
1106. GEORGE BLACKWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 2 sheets, value 4s., and 1 napkin, value 6d., the goods of Thomas Lawrence.
THOMAS LAWRENCE. I keep a house at No.3. Charles-court, St. Martin's-lane. I let it out to single men furnished; the prisoner took a lodging of me last Wednesday week, the 4th of April; he came the next day, and staid two nights - on Saturday morning I was awoke by one of my lodgers calling to him, and asking if that was his napkin which was hanging out of his pocket; I got up, but before I was dressed, he had got him back into my house, with the two sheets and the napkin.
THOMAS CURRY. About a quarter past five o'clock, on Saturday morning last, I heard the prisoner coming down stairs; I opened my room door, and bade him good morning; as he was going down the next flight of stairs I saw part of a napkin hanging out of his pocket - I opened the street door, and said, "Master, does that belong to you?" he turned and said, "No, no;" I followed him, and asked him again; he said, "Say nothing about it, I have got a sheet;" he took off his hat, and there was a sheet - he then went back; I called the prosecutor; he went up into his room, and I went up after him - he had his clothes unbuttoned; I said, "You have another sheet round your body," but I did not see it.
Prisoner. I had not got out of the court. Witness. Yes, you had, and ran - when I first spoke he did not make me any answer; I then spoke to him again, and he said, "No, no, we will go and have a little coffee."
THOMAS RYALL (Police-constable F 112.) I took the prisoner, and have the property.
GUILTY. Aged 25. - Confined Three Months.

108. ELEANOR HAMILTON was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of April, 1 morocco case, value 1s. 6d., and 12 pairs of ear-drops, value 4l. 18s., the goods of John Pierce.
CAROLINE PIERCE. I am the sister of John Pierce - we occupy a first floor in Rabere-street; I make child-bed linen. The prisoner was in my service; she left me without notice on the 2nd of April, and the next day I missed a dozen pairs of coral drops; my brother carves them - they were in a drawer in the room we worked; the prisoner did not sleep there.
JOHN PIERCE. I know these drops; they were in my care - I had them to carve.
RICHARD CHARLES HAMMOND. I am shopman to Mr. Edwards, a pawnbroker, at Hoxton. I have four pairs of coral drops, in a case, pawned by the prisoner, on the 2nd of April, about nine o'clock in the evening, for 4s.
MARY WELLINGS. The prisoner lodged with me; she came home on Monday night, the 2nd of April, and said she had got some coral drops to take to be carved for her master - she asked my daughter to go with her, and she went away directly; I did not see her again till Wednesday - I said, "What have you done?" she began to cry, and said she was very sorry - I said, "You had better go to the station-house, your master and mistress have been here;" she was in great distress.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury and Prosecutor.
Confined One Hour.

1110. HENRY JONES and SOPHIA MORGAN were indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of April. 1 box, value 1s.; 2 sheets, value 3l.; 2 pillow-cases, value 2s.; 6 tablecloths, value 10s.; 7 napkins, value 3s.; 13 towels, value 5s.; 3 shirts, value 6s.; 3 handkerchiefs, value 30s.; 5 pairs of stockings, value 5s.; 6 shifts, value 12s.; 1 habitshirt, value 1s., and 1 waistcoat, value 1s., the goods of Frederick Beauclerk, Esq., commonly called Lord Frederick Beauclerk; and HARRIET BIRD was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, well knowing them to have been stolen: against the Statute, &c.
ELIZABETH TIPPLE. I am school-room maid, in the service of Lord Frederick Beauclerk - he lives at St. Michael's, near St. Alban's. I packed up a box, containing the articles stated - they belonged to Lord Beauclerk's children; I saw the footman cord the box - it was to be sent by the carrier to Fitzroy-square, London; this is the box - I saw it afterwards at Marlborough-street; these are the articles - I have no doubt whatever of them; they are worth from 10l. to 15l.
BENJAMIN SCHOFIELD. I am an officer of Marlborough-street. On the 3rd of April I went with James Williams to No. 13, Hopkin-street - I saw a great light through the key-hole of a room on the first floor; I looked through the key-hole, and saw a box and some linen - I knocked at the door; it was not opened, but the light was put out - I heard the window move; I broke open the door, and went in; I saw Jones and Morgan standing in the room, and Bird getting under the bed - I asked how the box came there; they said George Falkingham had left it there, and that was all they knew about it; I found some duplicates in theroom - Bird said she had pawned the articles by order of Jones and George Falkingham; I found no duplicates on either of the prisoners - I found two shirts on the floor with the wristbands cut off and the collars cut down; I looked out at the window, and saw a barrow, which proved to be stolen; I do not think any one could have got out of the window, as I had men watching - part of the box was burning when I went into the room.
JAMES WILLIAMS. I am footman to Lady Dillon, who lives in Fitzroy-square. On the 3rd of April the St. Alban's carrier brought this box there - I took it in, and put it into the front hall; it was corded at the time - Morgan and Jones came there about one o'clock the same afternoon - I had not seen them before; Morgan gave me a bit of paper, and asked if it was right; I said Yes - she said she came from Mrs. Falkingham, and I helped her with the box to a barrow, which was standing at the door; when I got it there she asked if there was any basket; I said No- Jones then wheeled away the barrow, and I went in doors; I went with Schofield, about ten o'clock, and saw what he has stated; some of the things were on the floor and some in the box - the prisoners were all in the room; it was about half a mile from Fitzroy-square.
ANN FALKINGHAM. I am the wife of William Falkingham; we live in Swinnie-place, Lisson-grove - I am a laundress, and wash for Lord Beauclerk; the things are sent every week to Lady Dillon's, in Fitzroy-square; I never employed either of the prisoners to fetch any linen from there. I lost my barrow on Monday evening, the 2nd of April, from a garden, in front of my house; I had seen Jones before, but had no acquaintance with him.
Jones. I can prove that her son resides at Hopkins-street, and they were his things that were there. Witness. It is my son-in-law - I do not know where he lodges; his name is George.
EDWARD NATHAN. I am shopman to a pawnbroker, in Cambridge-street, Golden-square. I have a table-cloth, pawned by Bird - I had not known her before, but I am sure she is the woman; I lent 4s. on it, in the name of Elizabeth Reed, No. 20, Compton-street.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Jones' Defence The laundress' son made his escape out of window - I inquired of him for a lodging, as I was out of work; he gave me leave to lodge there two or three nights, and on the Tuesday, he asked me to go and fetch the box of linen for him, which he was in the habit of doing- I went there, and took the wheelbarrow, which he went and got from his mother's; Morgan went with me - I wheeled it to Warren-street, and he there took the barrow of me; I returned home in the evening about ten minutes before the officer came; when he heard them come up, he blew the light out himself, got out of the window, and through a dust-hole; I had no suspicion that any thing was wrong - he said he was going to take the things to his mother's the next day.
ANN FALKINGHAM. I had sent George for the linen about six weeks before this, but he has left me, and got into bad company.
Morgan's Defence. He asked me to go and get the linen, which I did.
Bird's Defence. George Falkingham asked me to pawn the things - I did not know they were stolen; he said they were his mother's.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 20.
MORGAN - GUILTY. Aged 16.
BIRD - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.

1111. LOUISA MANTCE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 1 watch, value 2l.; 2 seals, value 10s., and 1 watch-key, value 5s., the goods of Henry Head.

[trialAccount t18320405-309]
1112. JOHN MURPHY and JAMES JOHNSTONE were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 shovel, value 2s., the gooods of Charles Syred.
CHARLES SYRED. I am a gardener, and live in Richmond-street, Lisson-grove. I was getting some gravel at a house in South-bank on the 7th of April; I left my shovel while we carried some gravel in - my man said the shovel was stolen; I ran out, and saw the prisoners drawing a water-cart - I followed them, and saw the shovel in the cart; I followed on to Lisson-grove, where I saw the officer, and told him - I then turned, and saw Murphy putting the shovel further into the cart, more out of sight - the officer took them.
Murphy's Defence. A little boy put the shovel on the water-cart, and told us to take it to the yard.
Johnstone's Defence. We were going home a little before twelve o'clock - I kept hold of the handle of the truck, till the officer stopped us; I do not know what was in it.
CHARLES SYRED. Their faces were both turned from the truck when I saw them, and Murphy was the only one I saw handle the shovel; I said to him, "You have a shovel belonging to me;" he said, "I believe there is one:" I said, "You know there is, you have just put it out of sight."
DANIEL LOCK. (Police-constable D 42.) I was applied to by the prosecutor, and took the prisoners - this shovel was put into the place where the water is let in.
MURPHY - GUILTY. Aged 41.
Confined Six Months.
JOHNSTONE - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320405-310]
1113. MARY ANN WARD was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of April, 5 half-crowns, 3 shillings, and 1 sixpence, the monies of Thomas Brown, from his person.

[trialAccount t18320405-311]
1114. MORGAN O'BRYAN. was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 1 pair of boots, value 8s., the goods of John Howard.
GEORGE STONE. (Police-constable C 99.) On Saturday evening, the 31st of March, I was in St. Martin's-court, and saw the prisoner looking into Mr. Howard's shopwindow - I watched him for five minutes; he put his hand inside the shop door, and took up a pair of Blucher boots - he ran along towards the bottom of the court; I overtook him, and took them from his hand.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did he go insidethe shop? A. No - he took them by putting his hand inside the door: they were on a shelf.
SAMUEL COLLIS. I am assistant to Mr. John Howard, No. 36. St. Martin's-court - these are his boots.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going up the court, and saw them at the door - I took them up, and the officer took me.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 16. - Confined Three Weeks.
[trialAccount t18320405-312]
1115. MARGARET HUDDART PALMER was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of February, 1 silver spoon, value 4s., the goods of James Fogg.
JAMES FOGG. I am a Police-surveyor, and live in Martha-street, St. George's; I have employed the prisoner to make clothes for me. On Sunday, the 26th of February, she brought home a pair of trousers: I told her to take them back to alter - I had two silver tea-spoons on the sideboard, and when she was gone I missed one; I found it at a pawnbroker's - I met her on the Wednesday following, and asked what she had done with the spoon; she made no answer; I told her I found it was at the pawnbroker's, and asked for the duplicate; she said she had destroyed it.
WILLIAM COOMBE. I was shopman to Mr. Watts, of Hereford-place, Commercial-road. I have a spoon, pawned on the 27th of February, by the prisoner, for 2s.(Property produced and sworn to)
The prisoner pleaded poverty, and received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18320405-313]
1116. JAMES CHARLES PEARCE was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, 1 pair of boots, value 22s.; 2 coats, value 20s.; 2 jackets, value 6s.; 1 smockfrock, value 6d., and 1 waistcoat, value 1s., the goods of John Towersey.
JOHN TOWERSEY. I am a post-boy, and live in Oxfordshire. On the 4th of April I was at my master's stable at Tetsworth; I left the articles stated in the stable - I missed them the next morning; I mentioned my loss to John Berry, a post-boy, on his way to Uxbridge; I saw a waistcoat taken off the prisoner's back on the Friday following, before the Magistrate at Uxbridge - that was one that I had lost.
JOHN BERRY. In consequence of hearing of the loss I made inquiry at Uxbridge; I found the prisoner, and had him taken into custody - I found on him a pair of boots and a frock; these are them.
GEORGE JEWELL. I saw the prisoner at Uxbridge - he offered me this jacket; I bought it of him for 12s., with this waistcoat.
JOHN BROWN. I have a blue jacket which I bought on the 5th, of the prisoner, at Uxbridge, which is twenty-seven miles from where the prosecutor lives.
THOMAS ALMAN. I bought a coat of the prisoner for 16s. and a pot of beer, at Uxbridge.
WILLIAM INGRAM. I am an officer. I took the prisoner; he had a smock-frock and a pair of boots on - I found a stall-man, to whom the prisoner had sold a great coat; I said I would take it - the stallman said, "Let him give me my money, 4s. 6d.," and he gave it him out of his purse - I saw a piece of gold in his purse; I took him to the White Hart, and searched him, but I could not find any gold - I asked him to open his month, and I found this sovereign in it.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOHN TOWERSEY. I found these things on the Friday- they had been sold on the Thursday - I left them safe on the Wednesday night, at half-past eleven o'clock; we found 1l. 7s. 3d. on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming through Brentford on Wednesday morning; I met a man, and bought the things of him.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320405-314]
1116. WILLIAM DEAN was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 10 spoons, value 30s., the goods of Benjamin Best, his master.
BENJAMIN BEST. I am a coal-merchant - the prisoner was my errand-boy. I missed some spoons, and asked him about them; he denied it; I told him it would be better to tell the truth - I saw a spoon in Old-street, but could not swear to it.
HUGH FISHER STANGER. I bought a spoon of two men, but the prosecutor gave it back to me, and I melted it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320405-315]
1117. WILLIAM SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of March, 1 cloak, value 12s., and 1 shawl, value 3s., the goods of Timothy Kalahan.
ANN LYON. I lodge in Short's-gardens, Drury-lane. On the 4th of April I met the prisoner in Long-acre, and in consequence of information, I asked him for some duplicates; he said he was the wrong person, and he went on; I followed him, and said he had robbed Timothy Kalahan, and if he did not give me the duplicates, I would have him taken up - he gave me the duplicates, and I gave him into custody.
MARY KALAHAN. I am the wife of Timothy Kalahan; we live in Short's-gardens. The prisoner lodged with us; I found one of my boxes broken open, and missed a shawl and a cloak.
EDWARD NEWSHEAD. I am in the service of a pawnbroker, in Tothill-street. I have a cloak, pawned on the 14th of March, in the name of William Smith, by a person like the prisoner, but I cannot swear to him; this is the duplicate.
WILLIAM GEORGE FRY. I am in the service of Mr. Lorton, a pawnbroker. I have a shawl, but did not take it in; this is the duplicate of it.
JOSEPH MAYS (Police-constable E 63.) I took the prisoner, and got these duplicates from Lyon.
Prisoner's Defence. I worked as a carpenter for the prosecutor; he owed me a little money, and would not pay me - I pawned the things, and gave up the duplicates.
TIMOTHY KALAHAN. I had employed him, but I do not owe him a farthing.
GUILTY. Aged 26. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18320405-316]
1118. ELLEN CONNELLAN and WILLIAM WILSON were indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of March, 1 jacket, value 20s.; 1 waistcoat, value 7s., and 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of James Bradshaw, from his person.
JAMES BRADSHAW. I am a seaman, and lodge in Martha-street. On the night of the 22nd of March I went to the Crooked Billet; I saw Connellan there - I got drunk, and she took my clothes off me; I found myself the next morning at the Police-station - my jacket, waistcoat, and handkerchief were gone; I did not see Wilson - I took Connellan there to drink.
MATTHEW POULTER. I am in the service of a pawnbroker. I have a jacket and a waistcoat, pawned by Connettan, on the 22nd of March, in the name of Jane Smith, at near eight o'clock in the evening, for 17s.
GEORGE LIDDIARD. I keep the Crooked Billet at the corner of King David-lane. I saw the prosecutor at my house with Connellan; he got drunk, and fell asleep - I afterwards saw Connellan in company with Wilson and two others; the two prisoners went into the room where the prosecutor was asleep - I then missed the prisoner and the persons who came in with them; I found the prosecutor stripped of his jacket and waistcoat, and laying on the tap-room floor - I went after the prisoners; I took Connellan in three or four minutes - Wilson was with her, but he ran away.
Connellan. Wilson was not in my company. Witness. Yes; Connellan and another woman came about four o'clock- they staid some time and went out; Wilson and another man, and the two women came again - they called for some drink, and gave my boy a bad halfpenny, which he refused; Connellan then came out, and said it was one she had taken there that afternoon - the prisoners then went into the tap-room, where the prosecutor was; I went into my skittle-ground with a pot of beer, and on my return the prisoners were gone; I went out and saw them under a gas-lamp; they saw me, and Wilson ran off.
GEORGE PHILLIPS (Police-constable K 87.) I took Wilson; I told him it was for a robbery at the Crooked Billet, between him, Connellan, and two more; he said he had never seen the house, and should not know where to go to it - he made some resistance.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Wilson's Defence. I was on board the Repulse on the night of the robbery.
CONNELLAN - GUILTY. Aged 25.
WILSON - GUILTY. Aged 27.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18320405-317]
1119. DANIEL CLARK was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of March, 2 spoons, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Mary Smith.
SARAH DUCKETT. I live with Mrs. Mary Smith in Beaufort-row, Chelsea - she is a widow. On the 30th of March, about eleven o'clock, as I cleaned the kitchen window, I heard the back door shut, and saw the prisoner going out; he asked if we wanted any lemons, and he had one in his hand - I told him to go round to the back door and wait: he said, No, he would not wait there - I went out of the area, and he went out of the gate and ran; I ran and caught his coat - he struggled with me, and got away, leaving part of his coat in my hand; Mr. Hall took him, and took from his basket two tea-spoons, one silver and one pewter - they are my mistress', and I had seen them at nine o'clock that morning, in the kitchen - I have seen the prisoner selling lemons.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. He did not run away immediately. A. As soon as I got out of the area, he came towards me - I said, "Go round to the back door," he said, "No, I will wait here" - he then went out of the gate; when I went to him he ran.
WILLIAM HALL. I am an officer. On the 30th of March I was in Beaufort-row; I heard a cry of Thieves! and saw this witness holding the prisoner's coat; he got from her and ran towards Battersea-bridge - I pursued, and took him; I found these spoons in his basket.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18320405-318]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1120. JOHN MEAD and ELIZABETH DEVALLION were indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of February, 12 shirts, value 18s.; 2 shawls, value 6s.; 30 handkerchiefs, value 16s.; 6 pairs of stockings, value 4s.; 6 aprons, value 2s., and 1 table-cloth, value 6s., the goods of Henry Phillips.
MARTHA CONWAY. I was nursing at the prosecutor's, and washing there. I left some things in the yard, and missed them - I did not see the prisoner there.
EDWARD CHILD. I am a pawnbroker. I have a shirt, pawned by a boy, in the name of Mead, for 1s. 3d. - I am not certain who it was.
JAMES GRAHAM (Police-constable K 49). I took the prisoner; Mead said he took the property, and Devallion was with him - he said he pawned the shift.
Devaltion's Defence. The woman the clothes belong to, told me to go to the pawnbroker's and get an iron, and if we could not get one, to bring the whole chain of them.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320405-319]
1121. TIMOTHY MURPHY and MARY ALLEN were indicted for stealing, on the 4th of April, 1 hat, value 9s., and 1 handkerchief, value 1s., the goods of Charles Bland.
ROBERT HAINES. I keep an eating-house in High-street, Shadwell. The prisoners came there together at twelve o'clock at night, on the 4th of April; there were two others in company with them, and they called for four basins of soup - they sat in the box in which the prosecutor sat asleep; I noticed them - Allen came in with a straw hat on; he went out with a black one - I went to look at the prosecutor, who lodges with me, and his hat was gone from the table where it had been laying; I suspected the prisoners, and went after them - I saw an officer, and described the parties; he brought Allen back first, and then got Murphy - the prosecutor was still asleep.
CHARLES BLAND. I am a sailor. I was at the eating-house - I fell asleep. but I was not drunk; when I awoke my hat was gone, and my handkerchief, which had been in it - this is my hat; I did not see the prisoner.
WILLIAM REEVES (Police-constable K 152.) I had information of this, and went down a dark street - I saw Allen with her hand behind her; I asked what she had got - she said, "My husband's hat;" I said, "How came you to have it?" she said, "I have had a row with him, he threw his hat at me, and ran away;" I took her to theeating-house, with the straw hat which she had in her hand - we left the hat there; she asked me to let her go, and she would find the man - she went on, and I followed her; she called Tim - he said, "Is that you?" she said,"Yes, you have got me into a nice piece of trouble about this hat;" he said, "Where is my hat?" she said, "The Policeman has it:" she went up to him - I followed her, and seized them both; he had no hat on then, but Criper found this hat within a few yards of where I took him.
ROBERT CRIPER. I am a private watchman. At a quarter before one o'clock I was called to assist the officer, who had the prisoners in charge - we took them to the station; I then went to the lane again, and found this hat, but no handkerchief.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Murphy's Defence. I was going into the house, and this woman, who was a stranger, was going in; she asked me to treat her, and I did - we then went out, and had some words; I threw my hat at her, and never saw it again.
MURPHY - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Confined Six Weeks.
ALLEN - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320405-320]
1122. SARAH PERROTT was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 1 watch, value 30s., and 1 watch-key, value 3s., the goods of William Miller.
WILLIAM MILLER. I am employed on board a ship. I met the prisoner on London-bridge on the night of the 25th of February, a little before nine o'clock; we went into the Redcross, and sat some time - we came out towards the bridge again; we then went under one of the arches of the bridge - we staid there some time, and then she said she rather expected there was a Policeman at the corner; she went to see - I then missed my watch out of my pocket: I went out, but did not see her - I went on till I saw a watchman, and told him the case - he told me to go the Policeman; I am sure my watch had not fallen out.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe you had not much money? A. I had 25s. or 26s.; I had not paid her any thing - I was to have paid her, and should if she had not gone away - I did not give her my watch; it had no case to it - I was to have given her 1s. and something to drink; I might be a little the worse for drink - I cannot say how much I drank: I might be two hours in the public-house - I had pawned the case of my watch.
JAMES LAWSON. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner brought this watch on Monday morning, and wanted to pawn it - she said she had found it on London-bridge; I asked where the case was - she said her husband had broken it, and it was gone to be repaired; my lad then put a paper into my hand, that such a watch had been stolen - the prisoner said she would fetch her husband, and ran out; I pursued and took her.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18320405-321]
1123. MARY ANN PHILLIPS was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February 1 shawl, value 5s., the goods of Harriet Handerside.
HARRIET HANDERSIDE. On the 28th of February I was unfortunately in the station-house; I had a shawl on; the prisoner, who was a stranger to me, was there - I was in there for some hours, and while there I lost my shawl; I told the officer of it.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you walk to the station? A. I dare say I was taken there for protection, because I would not give my address; the prisoner was tipsy - I do not know which of us was the worst; I was sitting sleeping when I was robbed.
JAMES TITT (Police-contable G 7.) I took the prisoner down to Worship-street; I then heard of this robbery, and saw the corner of a shawl hanging below the prisoner's gown - I took her back to the prosecutrix, who was at the station; I asked her to describe the shawl, which she did, and when I showed it her, she claimed it.
Cross-examined. Q. Were not the prosecutrix and the prisoner both drunk? A. The prosecutrix was very drunk; the prisoner was not very drunk - she might have been drinking.
Prisoner's Defence. I certainly had the shawl somehow; but I was not in the least conscious of it till I was told of it.
NOT GUILTY.
